preference learned from data.\nIn the autoregressive era, GENRE~\\cite{decao2021genre} pioneers trie-based constrained beam search for autoregressive entity retrieval, restricting each decoding step to tokens consistent with valid entity names from a predefined catalog.\nGenIE~\\cite{josifoski2022genie} extends this approach to end-to-end information extraction with state-based constraints that enforce valid triple structures, achieving strong performance on closed-world knowledge base population, though it requires task-specific fine-tuning on the target schema.\nAdaKGC~\\cite{adakgc_emnlp2023} introduces schema-constrained dynamic decoding with trie-based prefix trees that adapt to the target schema, enforcing that generated entity and relation names belong to a predefined inventory, a vocabulary-level constraint that ensures name validity but does not verify type compatibility between a relation and its arguments.\nGCD~\\cite{geng2023gcd} provides a unified grammar-constrained decoding framework for structured NLP tasks including IE, introducing input-dependent grammars that adapt constraints based on the source text. GCD is the closest precursor to OntoCoDec in applying logits masking to IE, though limited to syntactic constraints.\nNeuroLogic Decoding~\\cite{lu2021neurologic} expresses lexical inclusion and exclusion constraints as predicate logic formulas enforced during beam search, offering a general mechanism for token-level control that can encode vocabulary membership but not relational type rules.\nThese approaches constrain \\emph{which tokens} can appear (vocabulary membership) but not \\emph{which type combinations} are valid for a given relation.\nOntoCoDec's semantic layer enforces relational constraints (e.g., that the head entity of relation $R$ must belong to type set $\\mathcal{D}(R)$) which cannot be expressed as static vocabulary restrictions because the valid set depends on the relation type generated earlier in the same sequence.\nWhile Roth \\& Yih's ILP formulation shares the same type-compatibility motivation, it operates as a post-hoc reranker over discrete candidates; OntoCoDec integrates enforcement directly into autoregressive decoding, preventing violations rather than filtering them.\n\n% ===========================================================================\n% Paradigm 3: Structural Constraints for IE\n% ===========================================================================\n\\paragraph{Structural Constraints via Training and Prompting.}\nAn orthogonal approach encodes IE output structure through model training or prompt design rather than decoding-time enforcement, relying on learned representations to produce schema-compliant outputs.\nUIE~\\cite{lu2022uie} defines a Structured Extraction Language and trains a unified text-to-structure model across 13 IE datasets, relying on large-scale pre-training to internalize output format constraints.\nGoLLIE~\\cite{sainz2024gollie} expresses annotation guidelines as Python dataclass definitions and fine-tunes Code-LLaMA to generate typed code, achieving strong zero-shot transfer to unseen IE schemas through the implicit type structure of programming languages.\nKnowCoder~\\cite{knowcoder_acl2024} takes this further by encoding 29K entity types from Wikidata as a Python class hierarchy, training a Code-LLM to generate structured extraction code that implicitly respects type inheritance, the most comprehensive schema-as-code approach to date.\nCodeIE~\\cite{li2023codeie} similarly reformulates IE as code generation, using the structural regularity of programming languages to reduce output format errors.\nADELIE~\\cite{qi2024adelie} uses SFT and DPO to align LLMs on IE tasks, achieving state-of-the-art results among open-source models while preserving general capabilities, though compliance with ontological constraints remains a learned soft preference rather than a guarantee.\nIn the supervised setting, joint entity-relation architectures such as SpERT~\\cite{eberts2020spert} and PFN~\\cite{yan2021pfn} enforce type consistency implicitly through shared representations: SpERT classifies entity spans and relation pairs in a single BERT pass with span-level negative sampling, while PFN decomposes feature encoding into entity-specific, relation-specific, and shared partitions via gating mechanisms.\nThese models achieve strong performance but are limited to fixed, predefined schemas and cannot generalize to unseen ontologies without retraining, precisely the ontology-portable, training-free scenario that OntoCoDec targets.\nEnriCo~\\cite{zaratiana2024enrico} applies Answer Set Programming (ASP) constraints during decoding of joint entity-relation extraction models, enforcing structural type compatibility at generation time. This is a decoding-time approach, though with model-specific, manually authored constraints rather than ontology-driven compilation.\nTraining-based methods provide \\emph{soft} structural guidance: the model learns to prefer valid outputs but cannot guarantee compliance at inference time, as demonstrated by non-trivial violation rates even in fine-tuned systems.\nImportantly, training-based and decoding-time approaches are complementary rather than competing: a system like GoLLIE could be combined with OntoCoDec's constraint layers, using fine-tuning to maximize the probability of valid outputs while decoding-time enforcement eliminates residual violations.\nOntoCoDec provides \\emph{hard} decoding-time enforcement that is training-free, automatically compiled from ontology schemas, and composable with any of these training-based approaches.\n\n% ===========================================================================\n% Paradigm 4: Type-Checking / Ontology Validation\n% ===========================================================================\n\\paragraph{Post-hoc Ontology Validation.}\nRather than constraining generation, several methods validate LLM outputs against ontological rules after generation, applying a detect-then-fix paradigm.\nODKE+~\\cite{khorshidi2025odke} dynamically generates ontology snippets containing domain/range constraints as prompt context, achieving 98.8\\% precision across 195 predicates at web scale, but constraint adherence relies on prompt-level guidance, not enforcement, leaving residual violations unaddressed.\nSPIRES/OntoGPT~\\cite{spires_2024} provides an ontology-based extraction framework that grounds LLM outputs against OBO ontologies post-generation, validating extracted entities and relations through ontology lookup and correcting mismatches in a second pass, representing the prompt-then-validate paradigm in the biomedical domain.\nOntoLogX~\\cite{ontologx_2025} applies SHACL validation to LLM-generated knowledge graphs, iteratively re-prompting the LLM to correct constraint violations and reducing them by approximately 10$\\times$ across multiple correction rounds. This demonstrates the detect-then-fix paradigm's effectiveness but also its inherent multi-pass cost.\n\\citet{upadhyay2023openie} inject entity-type knowledge as constraints into Open IE via both constrained inference and constraint-aware training, improving F1 by 29\\% on CaRB, though their constrained inference operates over a discrete candidate set rather than during autoregressive generation.\n\\citet{regino2025kgcurator} propose a multi-stage LLM pipeline for validating RDF triple insertions against RDFS domain/range constraints, where each validation step requires a separate LLM call, effective but computationally expensive at scale.\nRELATE~\\cite{olasunkanmi2025relate} addresses the alignment gap between LLM-extracted biomedical relations and standardized ontology predicates through a three-stage pipeline: ontology preprocessing with predicate embeddings, similarity-based retrieval via SapBERT, and LLM-based reranking with negation handling, achieving 94\\% accuracy@10 on ChemProt.\nWhile RELATE demonstrates effective ontology grounding for relation extraction, its constraints are applied \\emph{post-extraction} through retrieval and reranking rather than during generation, and it requires domain-specific embedding models trained on biomedical corpora.\nOntoCoDec enforces ontology constraints \\emph{during} autoregressive decoding and requires no additional trained components beyond the base LLM.\nConcurrently, \\citet{zhou2026ontologytools} compile OWL ontologies into executable tool interfaces within The World Avatar framework, requiring LLM agents to invoke ontology-aware tools that enforce semantic constraints during knowledge graph construction from scientific text.\nThis ``ontology-to-tools'' approach shares OntoCoDec's philosophy of compiling formal ontological knowledge into runtime enforcement mechanisms, but operates at the tool-call level (constraining which API functions the agent invokes) rather than at the token level (constraining which tokens the decoder produces), and requires an agentic multi-step workflow rather than single-pass autoregressive generation.\n\\citet{ekaw2024_ontology_beam} introduce ontology-constrained beam scoring for clinical summarization, applying soft biases (not hard masks) during beam search to steer outputs toward ontology-compliant terms, a middle ground between unconstrained generation and hard enforcement that reduces but cannot eliminate violations.\nPost-hoc validation detects violations after generation, requiring rejection or correction cycles that waste compute. In our setting, 15.37\\% of generated triples contain domain/range violations under unconstrained decoding, representing substantial wasted generation effort.\nOntoCoDec \\emph{prevents} violations during decoding, redirecting probability mass to valid alternatives rather than discarding invalid outputs, with only marginal overhead from logits masking compared to the cost of regenerating or correcting violated triples.\nSoft-constraint approaches such as ontology-weighted beam scoring reduce violation frequency but cannot eliminate it, whereas OntoCoDec substantially reduces violations (DR$\\downarrow$94\\% on SciERC, $\\downarrow$45--90\\% on DocRED depending on model).\n\n% ===========================================================================\n% Paradigm 5: Constrained Decoding Quality Concerns\n% ===========================================================================\n\\paragraph{Quality Impact of Constrained Decoding.}\nA growing body of work raises concerns that constrained decoding degrades LLM generation quality, particularly for reasoning-intensive tasks where format enforcement interferes with chain-of-thought processes.\n\\citet{tam2024letmespeak} conduct a comprehensive evaluation showing that rigid format constraints (JSON, XML) harm performance on math and multi-hop QA benchmarks but \\emph{benefit} classification-style tasks such as slot filling, a distinction directly relevant to IE, which is fundamentally an extraction (classification-like) task rather than a reasoning task.\nCRANE~\\cite{banerjee2025crane} provides theoretical analysis of why restricting outputs to narrow grammars suppresses chain-of-thought reasoning by eliminating intermediate reasoning tokens, and proposes an alternating strategy between unconstrained reasoning phases and constrained output phases to mitigate this effect.\n\\citet{schall2025hiddencost} conduct a systematic study across 11 models and multiple benchmarks, finding 10--30\\% semantic correctness degradation on reasoning tasks under structural constraints, with larger models showing greater absolute degradation.\n\\citet{zhou2026snowballing} identify ``structure snowballing,'' a failure mode where format compliance cognitive load traps models in repetitive structural patterns rather than improving reasoning during self-correction iterations.\nThese findings establish a legitimate concern for constrained decoding in reasoning-heavy applications.\nHowever, information extraction occupies a fundamentally different regime: the model extracts entities and relations from input text rather than generating novel reasoning chains, and \\citet{tam2024letmespeak}'s own results show that extraction-style tasks are robust to, or even benefit from, format constraints.\nOntoCoDec addresses this concern empirically: domain/range violations decrease by 94\\% on SciERC (15.37\\%$\\to$0.93\\%) and 45--90\\% on DocRED across two models, with F1-neutral impact on SciERC ($+$0.13pp, $p{=}0.58$) and significant R-F1 gains on DocRED (Qwen $p{<}0.001$, Llama $p{=}0.013$).\nCritically, OntoCoDec's constraints target only type tokens (entity types, relation labels), leaving free-text spans (entity surface forms and their boundaries) entirely unconstrained, which minimizes the distribution distortion that causes quality degradation in reasoning tasks.\nA two-pass variant (S2), explored in ablation (Section~5), separates extraction and constraint enforcement into distinct passes, analogous to CRANE's alternating strategy; however, our primary single-pass approach already avoids reasoning interference by constraining only type tokens while leaving free-text spans unconstrained.\n\n% ===========================================================================\n% Our Approach\n% ===========================================================================\n\\paragraph{Our Approach.}\nOntoCoDec occupies a unique position in this landscape: it is the first system to \\emph{automatically compile} IE ontology schemas, including domain/range axioms, into token-level semantic masks, extending grammar-constrained decoding from syntactic to semantic enforcement without manually authored rules.\nUnlike vocabulary-constrained methods that restrict individual tokens to a fixed set, OntoCoDec enforces \\emph{cross-field relational constraints} through a stateful entity registry that tracks extracted types during autoregressive generation, enabling dynamic constraint evaluation that depends on previously decoded content.\nThis approach is orthogonal to supervised IE methods and applicable to any autoregressive LM without model modification or additional training.\nUnlike training-based IE systems, OntoCoDec is training-free and substantially reduces type violations at inference time (DR$\\downarrow$94\\% on SciERC, $\\downarrow$45--90\\% on DocRED); unlike post-hoc validation, it prevents violations rather than detecting them, redirecting probability mass to valid alternatives with significant R-F1 gains on DocRED ($p \\leq 0.013$) and F1-neutral impact on SciERC.\nThe two constraint layers, grammar (structural) and semantic (relational), compose via bitwise AND with an enabling dependency: the grammar layer ensures every generated type token belongs to the valid vocabulary, which enables the semantic layer to look up and enforce domain/range rules for that type.\n",

```json
    }
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
I've now read the entire paper thoroughly. Let me compile my review. Let me organize my thoughts:

**Key findings from reference verification:**
- 65 bib entries, 56 unique cited keys, 8 orphan bib entries (duplicate entries that exist in slightly different key names)
- 1 duplicate key (schall2025hiddencost)
- 4 not_found online, 12 mismatches (mostly minor author name formatting)
- No missing bib entries - all cited keys have corresponding bib entries

**Summary of the paper:**
OntoCoDec is a two-layer constrained decoding framework that compiles IE ontology schemas into token-level masks:


- The grammar layer uses XGrammar-based context-free grammars to enforce valid JSON structure and vocabulary constraints
- The semantic layer applies domain and range type constraints through a stateful entity registry and dynamic logit masking

The evaluation spans three information extraction tasks: SciERC for relation extraction with 7 relations, DocRED for relation extraction with 96 relations, and WikiEvents for event extraction with 56 event types. They test across three base models (Qwen3-8B, Llama-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B) plus a 70B variant, evaluating both zero-shot and LoRA fine-tuned settings.

Now I'm ready to write a thorough review that examines the method's rigor and experimental design.

The strengths are clear: the two-layer architecture is well-formalized with explicit dependencies between layers, comprehensive ablations across both datasets, and validation spanning multiple models, datasets, tasks, and scales. The paper honestly quantifies the computational-fidelity tradeoff and provides extensive appendix material including reproducibility details, case studies, and sensitivity analyses. The constraint tightness analysis also grounds the approach theoretically.

For method rigor, the pair-based enforcement claim holds up mathematically—the footnote shows 33-37% spurious pairs under independent enforcement versus the stricter pair-based approach. The enabling dependency is critical: removing the semantic layer while keeping grammar causes a significant drop in detection rate, confirming that semantic constraints genuinely build on the grammar foundation.

The constraint composition formalism is straightforward—essentially bitwise AND operations—and while the grammar-first fallback is a reasonable design choice, the paper could be more explicit about its theoretical properties. The dual-factor pattern showing R²=0.886 is based on only four data points with marginal statistical significance (p≈0.058), so framing it as a "descriptive characterization" rather than a strong pattern is the right call.

On the experimental side, the WikiEvents test set is quite small—just 20 documents for evaluating composability—which leads to wide confidence intervals and limits how much we can generalize from those results.

The sample sizes across other datasets are similarly constrained: DocRED uses only 50 documents for beam search validation while SciERC uses the full 551, and the full DocRED sampling would require roughly 300 hours of computation, so they work with 114 documents instead. For the LoRA fine-tuning validation, they add full fine-tuning in the appendix but acknowledge the learning rate wasn't properly tuned, resulting in noticeably lower performance compared to their LoRA-optimized results.

The zero-shot R-F1 scores of 2-8% are quite low, though the paper frames this as intentionally stress-testing constraint robustness rather than optimizing for F1 directly—a reasonable argument, though a reviewer might question the practical relevance. The fine-tuned composability results at 40-46% R-F1 are more compelling. There's also an inconsistency in how they report constraint violations: they show CF violations drop to ≤1.2% after fine-tuning on RE, but for EE they only mention AC≤0.8% without clarifying what happens to CF on the fine-tuned EE model. Additionally, Mistral-7B shows quite different behavior compared to the other models tested.

Looking at the cross-dataset results, DocRED shows much less dramatic drops in detection rate compared to SciERC—only 45% versus 94%—which weakens the generalization claims somewhat, even though the tightness explanation makes sense. The statistical foundation also feels shaky: they fit a line through just 4 points with R²=0.886 and acknowledge p≈0.058, correctly labeling it descriptive but still concerning for a methods paper. Similarly, the Spearman correlation of -0.77 for the CF-DR trade-off is based on only 6 data points.

On the reproducibility side, they've been thorough with hyperparameters, prompts, and hardware specs disclosed in the appendices, though code isn't available yet. What's notably absent are comparisons with other schema-aware systems like GoLLIE, KnowCoder, EnriCo's constraint approach, BoostCD, and ODKE+—these would have strengthened the positioning considerably.

There are a couple of substantive concerns worth flagging: the constraint table relies on training-set co-occurrence patterns, so it'll inevitably miss rare but valid type combinations (they acknowledge this with the 33% schema coverage gap). And while they claim "near-lossless composability," that's based on R-F1 staying within ±0.6pp—but entity F1 actually varies significantly by model, ranging from +1.22 with Qwen to -0.64 with Llama on DocRED. The statistical rigor has some gaps too, particularly around the correlation claims and how they're interpreting the significance thresholds.

Looking at the overall picture, I'd lean toward a 3.5 or maybe even a 4 (Borderline Accept). The core contribution is genuinely novel—addressing type violations in LLM-based IE is a real problem—and the two-layer architecture is clean and theoretically motivated. The experimental scope is genuinely impressive across models and datasets, and they're refreshingly honest about what doesn't work. The practical results with fine-tuning are deployment-grade. The main limitations are real but somewhat incremental: the zero-shot composability-fidelity tradeoff is still there, some experimental subsets are quite small, and they're missing direct head-to-head comparisons with schema-aware baselines. These feel like things that don't fundamentally undermine the contribution.

I'll settle on 3.5 (Borderline Conference) as my final rating.
```

---

## Summary（2-3 句）

OntoCoDec 提出将 IE 本体的 domain/range 约束编译为两层 token-level decoding mask（grammar + semantic），在三个数据集、四个模型上验证了约束有效性和与 LoRA 微调的可组合性。核心思路清晰、方法形式化合理、实验覆盖面广，但若干关键统计声明基于极小样本（n=4/6），CF trade-off 在 zero-shot 下仍显著（最高 21%），部分实验子集过小（WikiEvents FT 仅 20 篇，DocRED beam 仅 50 篇），缺少与 schema-aware IE baseline（GoLLIE、KnowCoder、BoostCD）的直接对比。

---

## Strengths

**S1. 问题定义清晰且有实际意义。** 论文精确定义了四类 violation（DR/RV/ET/CF），并在 §5 Setup 中量化了 baseline violation 率（SciERC 15.37%, DocRED 18.46%），说明即便微调后仍有 1-2% 残留。这为约束方法建立了明确的 motivation。

**S2. 两层架构的形式化和 enabling dependency 论证充分。** §3 的 pair-based sequential masking 是正确的：论文在 footnote 中给出非矩形签名的量化证据（SciERC 0/7 rectangular, DocRED 23/96 rectangular, 33-37% 虚假 type pair），证明 independent enforcement 不够。Semantic-only 仅降 24% DR vs Full S1 降 94%（Table 1），实验上验证了 enabling dependency。

**S3. 实验覆盖面广。** 3 数据集（SciERC/DocRED/WikiEvents）× 4 模型（Qwen3-8B/Llama-3.1-8B/Mistral-7B/Llama-70B）× 2 设定（zero-shot/LoRA）× 3 解码策略（greedy/sampling/beam）。跨任务泛化（RE→EE, §5.4）无需架构修改。这在 constrained decoding for IE 工作中属于较完整的验证。

**S4. CF↔DR trade-off 的诚实讨论。** 论文没有回避 CF 上升问题（zero-shot 最高 12.02% RE、21% EE），在 Appendix B 中提供了 CF fallback 消融（Table 7）、CF 分布分析（Table 8）、beam search 作为缓解路径的定量评估。CF 双机制分析（registry override vs grammar-first fallback）有启发性。

**S5. 可重复性信息充分。** Appendix G 给出完整的 LoRA 超参、训练步数、硬件、软件版本；Appendix A 给出完整 prompt；Appendix H 给出 prompt sensitivity 分析（DR reduction 标准差仅 0.3pp across 3 prompts）。

**S6. Constraint tightness 概念。** §4 定义的 κ(r) = 1 − |V(r)|/|E|² 提供了一个有用的分析框架，解释了 SciERC（κ̄=0.333, DR↓94%）与 DocRED（κ̄=0.842, DR↓45%）之间的差异。

---

## Weaknesses

**W1 [Must-Fix] 关键统计声明基于极小样本，过度解读。**
- §4 dual-factor pattern 的线性拟合 R²=0.886 基于 n=4 数据点（df=2, p≈0.058）。论文在 Appendix B 中确实承认这是"descriptive characterization rather than a confirmatory statistical test"，但在 §4 正文中仍将其作为核心分析框架呈现（"This dual-factor pattern ... predicts the cross-dataset results"）。用 n=4 的拟合来"predict"结果，措辞过强。
- CF↔DR trade-off 的 Spearman ρ=-0.77, n=6：在 n=6 时，α=0.05 双尾检验的临界值约为 0.886，ρ=-0.77 **不显著**。但论文将其作为"inversely associated"的证据，未报告 p-value。
- **建议**：(1) 正文中将 dual-factor 降级为"observed pattern consistent with"而非"predicts"；(2) 报告 Spearman 检验的 p-value 并明确承认不显著；(3) 增加更多 model×dataset 配置以提高统计效力，或移除定量 correlation 声明，仅保留定性描述。

**W2 [Must-Fix] WikiEvents fine-tuning composability 的 test set 仅 20 篇文档，统计效力严重不足。**
- §5.4: "LoRA extends composability to EE (20-document test set)"。论文自己也承认"wide confidence intervals at this sample size"。
- Trigger F1 shift -5.4/-0.6pp 在 20 篇上完全可能是噪声。这是 EE composability 的唯一验证，支撑了 Abstract 和 Contribution 4 中的 claim（"RoV→0% and AC≤0.8% on EE"）。
- **建议**：(1) 报告 bootstrap CI 和 p-value；(2) 增大 test set（WikiEvents 完整 test set 有多少？246 zero-shot 已用，为何 fine-tuning 仅 20？）；(3) 若无法扩大，在 Abstract 中明确标注 sample size caveat。

**W3 [Should-Fix] 缺少与 schema-aware IE baseline 的直接实验对比。**
- Related Work（§6）讨论了 GoLLIE、KnowCoder、GCD、BoostCD、EnriCo 等，但 **无一在实验中作为 baseline**。唯一的"baseline"是 unconstrained decoding + grammar-only + semantic-only + post-hoc filtering。
- GoLLIE（ICLR 2024）通过 schema-as-code 在 zero-shot IE 上取得了强结果，且也能在 SciERC/DocRED 上评估。BoostCD（EMNLP 2025）直接在 grammar-constrained IE 上做了 boosting，是最直接的竞争者。
- **建议**：至少与 GCD 和一个 training-based schema-aware 系统（GoLLIE 或 KnowCoder）做 violation rate 对比，以回答"现有方法到底留了多少 violation？OntoCoDec 的增量在哪里？"

**W4 [Should-Fix] DocRED 上 zero-shot DR 仅降 45%（Qwen），10.21% violation 残留；论文对此的解释虽合理但结论表述需调整。**
- Abstract 报告 "DR drops by 42–99%"，但 42% 对应 DocRED Mistral（从 57.42% 降到 33.20%），实际还有 33% 残留。对 end user 而言，三分之一的 triple 仍有 type violation 是不可接受的。
- 论文用 constraint tightness 解释，但这意味着方法在 tight-constraint 数据集上效果有限——恰恰是约束最有价值的场景（因为 tight constraint 意味着 violation 更容易传播到下游）。
- **建议**：在 Abstract/Conclusion 中更谨慎地表述 zero-shot 场景的局限性，不要让 "42-99%" 的表述给读者留下"大多数情况下接近 99%"的印象。

**W5 [Should-Fix] CF violation 在 EE 上高达 21%（zero-shot），论文对 fine-tuned EE 的 CF 缺乏报告。**
- §5.4 WikiEvents fine-tuned 结果仅报告 "RoV→0%" 和 "AC≤0.8%"，但未报告 CF 率。RE 上 fine-tuning 将 CF 从 9.90% 降到 1.18%（SciERC），但 EE 的 CF 是否也同样降低？
- EE 的 CF 在 zero-shot 下比 RE 更高（15-21% vs 10-12%），fine-tuned EE 的 CF 数据对完整性至关重要。
- **建议**：补充 WikiEvents fine-tuned 的 CF 数据。

**W6 [Should-Fix] Beam search 验证不均衡：SciERC 用完整 551 doc，DocRED 仅 50 doc 子集。**
- Table 10（Appendix D）：SciERC beam search 在 551 篇上评估，但 DocRED 仅用 50 篇 matched subset。Abstract 中"beam search (k=4) eliminates all four violation types under fine-tuned beam decoding (SciERC, both Qwen3-8B and Llama-3.1-8B)" 正确指明了 SciERC，但读者自然期望 DocRED 也有同等验证。
- 50 篇的 beam search 结果中 parse failure 升到 6%（3/50），在小样本上不够可靠。
- **建议**：说明为何 DocRED beam search 只用 50 篇（计算成本？）；增大子集或明确标注置信度。

**W7 [Should-Fix] 约束表基于 training-set 频率统计，引入了不可消除的 schema coverage gap。**
- Appendix B residual DR 分析显示 33% 的残留 violation 来自 "schema coverage gaps"——频率表未覆盖 rare-but-valid type pairs。这意味着约束表本身不完整。
- 论文称这是"irreducible by decoding-time constraints alone"，但实际上如果使用完整的 ontology schema（而非频率统计的子集）来构建约束表，这些 gap 是可以消除的。
- **建议**：解释为何不直接使用 ontology 的完整 domain/range 规范而要依赖频率统计。如果是因为数据集的 ontology 不完整（如 DocRED 无显式 domain/range 声明），需要明确说明。

**W8 [Minor] bib 中存在 8 个 orphan 条目和 1 个 duplicate key。**
- 引用验证发现 8 个 orphan bib entries（定义了但未被引用的条目），经检查它们是同一论文的 duplicate keys（如 `boostcd2025` 和 `sakota2025boostcd`）。`schall2025hiddencost` 也有 duplicate。
- 2 篇论文未在 OpenAlex 找到（`vukovic2024dore`、`regino2025kgcurator`）——可能是非常新的论文。

**W9 [Minor] Limitations section 信息密度极高但缺乏结构化。**
- 当前 Limitations section 是一个超长段落，涵盖 CF 影响、Entity F1 影响、zero-shot 局限、fine-tuning 验证范围、scalability、beam search 等。按 ARR 要求应更易读。
- **建议**：分条列出或至少分段。

---

## Questions

**Q1.** Constraint table（σ(r)）是如何构建的？是从 ontology 的官方 domain/range 声明中提取，还是从训练集共现统计中推断？如果是后者，为何不使用完整 ontology specification？

**Q2.** WikiEvents fine-tuning 的 test set 为何仅 20 篇？WikiEvents 完整 test set 有多少篇？是否有计算资源限制或数据可用性问题？

**Q3.** Grammar-first fallback 率在 Llama SciERC 上高达 36.6%（Appendix B），这意味着超过三分之一的 type-bearing position 放弃了 semantic enforcement。作者是否考虑过其他 fallback 策略（如 relaxing to top-k semantic candidates 而非完全放弃 semantic mask）？

**Q4.** 论文声称"OntoCoDec is training-free"（§6, Appendix B），但约束表的构建是否需要训练数据中的共现统计？如果需要，"training-free"的边界需要更精确。

**Q5.** DocRED 使用 dev set 而非 test set 评估（因 test 标注不公开），这是否会导致与使用 test set 的现有工作不可比？

**Q6.** 70B 实验仅在 zero-shot 下验证（§5.5），为何不做 70B + LoRA？这将是 composability 最有说服力的验证。

---

## Missing References

1. **BoostCD (Sakota et al., EMNLP 2025)**: 在 bib 中存在但仅在 Related Work §6 列举式引用，未讨论其与 OntoCoDec 的关键区别（boosting constrained + unconstrained decoding 的组合策略可能缓解 CF trade-off）。
2. 缺少与 **TANL/REBEL** 的 violation rate 对比讨论——这些 seq2seq IE 方法的 type violation 率是多少？
3. 缺少 **SuRE (Structured Reasoning Enhancement)** 等近期工作对 constrained decoding 在 extraction 任务上效果的讨论。

---

## Overall Assessment

**Overall**: 3.5（Borderline Conference）
**Soundness**: 3.5/5
**Excitement**: 3/5
**Confidence**: 4/5

**理由**：

论文的核心贡献——将 IE ontology domain/range 约束编译为 token-level semantic mask——是一个清晰、有用的技术贡献。两层架构的设计和 enabling dependency 的论证在方法层面令人信服。实验覆盖了多个模型、数据集和任务，overall experimental effort 值得肯定。CF trade-off 的诚实讨论和详尽的附录也是加分项。

主要顾虑集中在：(1) 核心统计声明（dual-factor R²、Spearman ρ）基于过小的样本量，影响了分析框架的可信度；(2) WikiEvents fine-tuning 仅 20 篇 test set，EE composability 的 evidence 薄弱；(3) 缺少与现有 schema-aware IE 方法的直接实验对比，难以判断增量贡献的大小；(4) DocRED zero-shot DR 仅降 45%，在 tight-constraint 场景下效果有限。这些问题中，W1 和 W2 需要在提交前修复以达到 Conference 级别，W3 的缺失使 novelty 评估困难。

## Step 2 — 交叉分享：请回应 Reviewer 2 的审稿意见

---

### Reviewer 2 审稿意见原文

**Overall: 3.5（Borderline Conference）| Soundness 4/5 | Excitement 3/5 | Reproducibility 5/5**

**Summary**: 本文提出 OntoCoDec，将 IE ontology schema 编译为两层 token-level 解码约束。在 SciERC、DocRED（RE）和 WikiEvents（EE）上跨 4 个模型评估，zero-shot DR 降低 42-99%，与 LoRA 组合后 DR ≤ 0.20% 且 R-F1 波动 < 0.6pp。实验全面但核心技术新颖性有限（code domain 类型约束解码的迁移），CF trade-off 削弱 zero-shot 故事。

**Strengths**:
- S1: 问题定义清晰，violation 分类体系完整
- S2: Enabling dependency 有实验支撑（semantic-only 24% vs full 94%）
- S3: Fine-tuning composability 是最强贡献（DR ≤0.20%, F1 neutral）
- S4: 实验覆盖面异常全面（3 datasets × 4 models × 多 decoding strategies）
- S5: Limitations 处理诚实透明
- S6: 可复现性极佳

**Weaknesses**:

**W1 [Should-Fix]** 核心技术相对 code domain type-constrained decoding 的增量贡献有限。核心机制（类型约束→token masks）在 PICARD、Synchromesh、MGD、Mündler et al. (PLDI 2025) 已充分验证。IE 特有贡献（dynamic entity registry, pair-based sequential masking）属于适配而非根本性算法创新。"Enabling dependency" 本质上是一个 obvious 的架构设计选择。

**W2 [Should-Fix]** Constraint tightness (κ) 分析价值被高估。κ 是直接归一化，dual-factor 拟合仅 n=4 (p≈0.058)，但 Contribution #1 用 causal language "driven by"。SciERC/DocRED DR 相近（15.37% vs 18.46%）可能是巧合。

**W3 [Should-Fix]** Zero-shot framing 与实际价值不一致。论文最强结果（fine-tuning composability）被埋在 zero-shot 分析之后。Zero-shot 下 total violation burden (DR+CF) 从 15.45% 降到 10.83%（SciERC），净减少仅 ~30%。

**W4 [Should-Fix]** Appendix Extended Related Work 中 AI 写作痕迹密集（-ing 分词短语 10+ 次，", confirming" 14 次）。

**W5 [Should-Fix]** WikiEvents fine-tuning 评估仅 20 文档。

**W6 [Should-Fix]** Abstract 数字堆砌（~15 个数字），信息密度过高。

**W7 [Minor]** Contribution #3 和 #4 高度重叠，可合并。

**W8 [Minor]** Table 3 Grammar-only 行 R-F1/E-F1 标粗叙事矛盾。

**W9 [Minor]** DocRED beam search 仅 50 文档子集。

**Questions**:
- Q1: CF↔DR total violation burden 净变化？Pareto 分析？
- Q2: WikiEvents FT 为什么只 20 文档？
- Q3: 能否 single integrated layer 避免 enabling dependency？
- Q4: WikiEvents 的 κ 值和预测力？
- Q5: 完整 ontology schema vs frequency-based 约束表能否消除 coverage gap？

**Missing References**: Hokamp & Liu (2017), DEGREE (Hsu et al., 2022), Roth & Yih (2004) 移入正文。

---

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Let me carefully review each of Reviewer 2's weaknesses and prepare my responses. I need to cross-reference with what I found in the paper to determine whether I agree or rebut each point.

Let me go through each weakness:

**W1**: Core technical novelty relative to code domain type-constrained decoding is limited. The mechanism (type constraints → token masks) is well-established in PICARD, Synchromesh, MGD, Mündler et al. The IE-specific contributions (dynamic entity registry, pair-based sequential masking) are adaptations not fundamental algorithmic innovations. "Enabling dependency" is essentially an obvious architectural choice.
 
The paper does address this in §3 and Appendix B, highlighting that entity types are inferred from free text rather than declared upfront, the registry builds dynamically during generation, and constraints adapt based on context. The empirical gap between semantic-only (24%) and full approach (94%) supports the enabling dependency's value. That said, R2 has a fair point—the core paradigm of type system → automaton → logit mask does originate from code domain work, and while the IE adaptations are genuine, whether they're substantial enough for a top venue is genuinely debatable. I think I should partially concede here.

**W2**: Constraint tightness (κ)

The analysis feels overstated given that κ is just a normalization factor and the dual-factor fit only has n=4 (p≈0.058). The causal framing in Contribution #1—"driven by"—is too strong for this evidence. The similar results on SciERC/DocRED (15.37% vs 18.46%) could plausibly be coincidental, though I'd push back slightly: the constraint tightness framework does offer a plausible mechanism even if the statistical support is weak. The core concern about overstating causality is valid.

**W3**: Zero-shot framing vs actual value mismatch

The strongest result—fine-tuning composability—gets buried after the zero-shot analysis, which feels like a framing issue. The total violation burden drops from 15.45% to 10.83% when moving beyond zero-shot, suggesting the real gains come from fine-tuning rather than the zero-shot setup the paper emphasizes.

**W4**: Potential AI writing traces in the appendix with repeated patterns like "-ing" participles and confirmation phrases. I didn't verify this systematically, but it's a fair observation about writing conventions, and since it's in the appendix rather than the main text, it's less consequential. I'll acknowledge the point.

**W5**: The WikiEvents fine-tuning evaluation uses only 20 documents, which I already flagged myself as a weakness.

**W6**: The abstract is information-dense with roughly 15 numbers packed in—violation rates, F1 scores, percentage improvements, p-values, and beam search metrics. While quantitative abstracts are standard in NLP systems papers, the density here is notable, though not necessarily a critical flaw.

**W7**: Contributions 3 and 4 do share some conceptual territory—both discuss composability with LoRA and constraint enforcement, with contribution 3 covering the performance trade-offs across models and datasets, while contribution 4 focuses on the specific constraint-violation trade-off and beam search solutions. There's meaningful overlap worth clarifying.

**W8**: Looking at Table 3, the Grammar-only row does show the highest R-F1 and E-F1 values, yet the narrative emphasizes DR reduction rather than these F1 gains, creating a disconnect between what the bolding highlights and what the text emphasizes.

**W9**: The beam search experiment on DocRED only covers a 50-document subset, which I'd already noted in my own review.

Now I'll draft the responses.
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_017AF1cs9d8RYB4sLDEYGVHj`</summary>

```text
1	\begin{abstract}
2	We present \textsc{OntoCoDec}, which compiles Information Extraction (IE) ontology schemas into two composable token-level decoding constraints that enforce structural and type-validity constraints during autoregressive generation:
3	(1)~a \emph{grammar layer} (XGrammar context-free grammar) that guarantees JSON structural validity and entity/relation vocabulary membership (ET and RV violations $\to$\,0\%); and
4	(2)~a \emph{semantic layer} that enforces per-triple domain/range type constraints via a stateful entity registry and dynamic logit masking, with grammar-first fallback when constraints conflict.
5	%
6	Without constraints, Large Language Model (LLM) based IE produces systematic domain/range (DR) violations: 15.37\% on SciERC (7 relations) and 18.46\% on DocRED (96 relations), with 1--2\% persisting even after fine-tuning; rates are driven by \emph{constraint tightness} rather than ontology or model scale.
7	Composed with Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) fine-tuning, \textsc{OntoCoDec} reduces DR to $\leq$\,0.20\% with R-F1 within $\pm$0.6\,pp of unconstrained baselines (R-F1 40--46): near-lossless composability at deployment-grade extraction quality.
8	% exp_ids: w5_lora_scierc_constrained_eval, w5_qwen_docred_lora_constrained_eval, w5_llama_lora_scierc_constrained_eval, w5_llama_docred_lora_constrained_eval
9	Under zero-shot decoding, which stress-tests constraint robustness at low extraction quality (R-F1 2--8\%), DR drops by 42--99\% across three model families (Qwen3-8B, Llama-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B) with no significant F1 cost (DocRED: $+$0.50\,pp, $p{<}0.001$; \S\ref{sec:cross-model}).
10	With beam search ($k{=}4$), the full stack eliminates all four violation types under fine-tuned beam decoding (SciERC, both Qwen3-8B and Llama-3.1-8B) while preserving extraction quality (R-F1 44.23 and 46.11, respectively); in zero-shot beam decoding, DR reaches 0\% for calibrated models on both RE datasets.
11	The framework generalizes to event extraction (WikiEvents: 56 event types, 80 roles) without architectural modification and eliminates event-vocabulary and role-vocabulary violations ($\to$ 0\%); where unconstrained outputs are severely off-schema, format compliance additionally enables entity extraction (Entity F1: 0\%\,$\to$\,12--18\%).
12	Fine-tuning composability extends to EE: LoRA + S1 eliminates RoV and reduces AC to $\leq$\,0.8\% on WikiEvents.
13	Per-slot enforcement introduces a cross-field consistency trade-off (up to 12\% on RE and 21\% on EE in zero-shot, resolved to $\leq$1.2\% by fine-tuning on RE).
14	OntoCoDec demonstrates that ontology schemas can be compiled into decoding-time constraints that compose losslessly with standard fine-tuning and generalize across four models spanning three tokenizer families and two model scales (8B to 70B). The framework bridges the gap between flexible generative extraction and the type-safety requirements of downstream knowledge systems.
15	\end{abstract}
16	
```

```text
1	% Section 1: Introduction (compressed, 669→~550 words)
2	
3	\section{Introduction}
4	\label{sec:introduction}
5	
6	Large language models are increasingly used for information extraction~\cite{li2023codeie,sainz2024gollie,wei2023zeroshot}, but when generating relation triples $(h, r, t)$, neither entity is guaranteed to carry a type consistent with the ontology's domain/range constraints for $r$.
7	We call such outputs \emph{domain/range violations}: structurally valid triples that are ontologically invalid.
8	Violations persist across the capability spectrum: 15.4\% of triples on SciERC~\cite{luan2018scierc} and 18.5\% on DocRED~\cite{yao2019docred} under zero-shot decoding, and 1--2\% even after LoRA fine-tuning, driven by \emph{constraint tightness} (few valid type pairs per relation) rather than ontology or model scale.
9	Constraint enforcement matters most at deployment-grade quality (R-F1 40--46\%): at this level, residual type violations propagate directly into knowledge graphs and decision-support pipelines; reliable type-safety enforcement is needed.
10	
11	Existing approaches leave a fundamental gap.
12	Schema-informed prompting reduces violations by 62\% on average but is per-relation unreliable (violations for \textsc{Evaluate-for} actually \emph{increase} by 5.8\,pp).
13	Grammar-constrained decoding~\cite{dong2025xgrammar,willard2023outlines,beurer-kellner2023lmql} guarantees syntactic well-formedness but cannot encode cross-field semantic constraints.
14	Post-hoc filtering achieves higher F1 in most zero-shot settings via precision gains, but at the cost of discarding up to 48\% of predictions including true positives; at high rejection rates (e.g., Mistral DocRED 73.7\%), it discards the majority of outputs and becomes impractical.
15	OntoCoDec preserves recall by enforcing constraints during generation rather than discarding non-compliant outputs.
16	Training-based approaches~\cite{sainz2024gollie,lu2022uie} provide only soft guidance that cannot eliminate violations.
17	
18	We propose \textbf{OntoCoDec} (\textbf{Onto}logy-\textbf{Co}mpiled \textbf{Dec}oding), a framework that compiles an IE ontology into two composable layers of decoding-time constraints.
19	Our two-layer architecture reflects the observation that structural violations (malformed output, unknown types) and semantic violations (type-constraint breaches) require fundamentally different enforcement mechanisms.
20	The \emph{grammar layer} enforces JSON well-formedness and vocabulary validity via XGrammar.
21	The \emph{semantic layer} maintains a stateful entity registry during generation: as entities are decoded, the registry records type assignments; when relation arguments are generated, dynamic logit masking restricts output to types compatible with the relation's domain/range specification.
22	The layers compose via bitwise AND with an \emph{enabling dependency}: the grammar layer guarantees vocabulary membership, so the semantic layer can look up domain/range rules.
23	Unlike type-constrained code generation where types are statically declared, IE requires a dynamic entity registry built during generation, with constraint targets that depend on the co-occurring relation.
24	Composed with LoRA fine-tuning, OntoCoDec reduces DR to $\leq$\,0.20\% at $<$0.6\,pp F1 cost (R-F1 40--46); under zero-shot decoding (R-F1 2--8\%), which stress-tests constraint robustness, DR drops by 94\% on SciERC while remaining F1-neutral.
25	
26	Our contributions:
27	\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]
28	    \item A systematic study of ontological constraint violations in LLM-based IE: rates are driven by constraint tightness rather than ontology scale, and prompt-level intervention is per-relation unreliable.
29	    \item OntoCoDec, a two-layer framework compiling ontology schemas into stateful logit masks that enforce pair-based domain/range constraints during autoregressive IE, unlike code generation where types are statically declared.
30	    \item Near-lossless composability with LoRA across RE and EE: DR\,$\leq$\,0.20\% at $<$0.6\,pp F1 cost on RE, RoV$\to$0\% and AC$\leq$0.8\% on EE. Zero-shot robustness across four models (8B--70B) and three datasets: DR $\downarrow$42--99\%, WikiEvents violations $\to$0\%; 70B achieves DR$\to$0\% on both RE datasets.
31	    \item Analysis of the CF$\leftrightarrow$DR trade-off in per-slot enforcement (resolved to CF\,$\leq$\,1.2\% by fine-tuning); beam search ($k{=}4$) eliminates all four violation types under fine-tuned decoding (SciERC, both Qwen3-8B and Llama-3.1-8B).
32	\end{enumerate}
```

_source line 124 · 2026-05-19T06:33:23.789Z_

以下是我对 Reviewer 2 每条 Weakness 的逐条回应。

---

**[AGREE] R2-W1: 核心技术相对 code domain type-constrained decoding 的增量贡献有限**

同意 R2 的核心判断。论文在 §3 和 Appendix B Extended Related Work 中明确承认了与 code domain 的关系（"the closest mechanistic analog to OntoCoDec, applying the same 'type system → automaton → logits mask' pipeline"），并列出了三个 IE-specific 差异（dynamic entity registry、free-text type inference、context-dependent constraint targets）。这些差异是真实的工程挑战，但从算法层面看确实更接近 domain adaptation 而非 fundamental innovation。

不过，我认为 R2 对"enabling dependency 是 obvious 的架构选择"这一判断稍显过强。Table 1（SciERC ablation）中 semantic-only 仅降 DR 24% 而 full S1 降 94% 的实验差距（§5.1），以及 Appendix B 中 Llama 36.6% grammar-first fallback 率的分析，说明这个 dependency 虽然事后看显而易见，但如果不做这个 ablation，一个 reasonable reviewer 完全可能认为 semantic layer 单独就够用。该消融的价值在于定量拆解，而非概念上的意外性。

总体而言，novelty 确实是本文的相对弱项。论文的主要价值更在于 systematic empirical study 和 composability verification，而非算法创新。

---

**[AGREE] R2-W2: Constraint tightness (κ) 分析价值被高估**

同意，且与我自己的 W1 高度一致。两个具体问题：

1. Contribution #1（§1, line 28）使用 "rates are driven by constraint tightness rather than ontology scale"——这是因果性语言，但 SciERC 和 DocRED baseline DR 相近（15.37% vs 18.46%）可能有多种解释（如 prompt 效果、模型 calibration、数据集 annotation 差异），不能仅凭两个数据点排除 ontology scale 的影响。
2. Dual-factor 拟合 R²=0.886 with n=4 在 Appendix B 中已标注 p≈0.058，但在 §4 正文中仍作为 predictive framework 呈现。

补充 R2 未提到的一点：SciERC/DocRED DR 相近但 **constrained 后 residual DR 差异极大**（0.93% vs 10.21%），这才是 tightness 真正有解释力的地方——但论文没有充分区分 baseline DR 的驱动因素和 residual DR 的驱动因素。

---

**[REBUT] R2-W3: Zero-shot framing 与实际价值不一致**

部分反驳。R2 的计算是正确的：SciERC zero-shot total violation burden (DR+CF) 从 15.45% 降到 10.83%，净减少 ~30%。但这个计算隐含了 DR 和 CF 的等权假设，而论文在 §4 中明确区分了两者的严重程度：

> "a DR violation renders the triple ontologically invalid ... whereas a CF violation means each triple is individually schema-valid but the same entity receives different type assignments across triples"

DR violation 使 triple **不可用**（downstream KG 拒绝该 triple），CF violation 使 triple **individually valid but globally inconsistent**（可修复、且在 fine-tuning 下降到 1.18%）。将两者简单相加会低估 DR 消除的实际价值。

不过，R2 关于"最强结果被埋在 zero-shot 之后"的 presentation 批评是合理的。论文确实可以重组叙事，将 fine-tuning composability（§5.1 最后一段 + Table 2）提前。当前结构先花大量篇幅在 zero-shot ablation（R-F1 仅 2-8%），再在一个 paragraph 中报告 fine-tuning 结果，叙事重心与实际贡献重心不匹配。

---

**[AGREE] R2-W4: Appendix Extended Related Work AI 写作痕迹密集**

同意。我在初审时未逐句检查 Appendix B Extended Related Work 的写作风格，但 R2 的观察（", confirming" 出现 14 次、-ing 分词短语 10+ 次）与项目写作规范中 §5.2-5.4 的禁忌直接冲突。这虽然是附录而非正文，但审稿人对此类 pattern 敏感，且 ARR 审稿时附录也在评审范围内。需要系统性清理。

---

**[AGREE] R2-W5: WikiEvents fine-tuning 评估仅 20 文档**

完全同意，与我自己的 W2 一致。20 篇 test set 上 trigger F1 shift -5.4pp 的 CI 必然极宽。这支撑了 Abstract 中 "Fine-tuning composability extends to EE: LoRA + S1 eliminates RoV and reduces AC to ≤0.8% on WikiEvents" 的 claim。如果 WikiEvents 完整 test set 有 246 篇（zero-shot 已用这个数量），那 fine-tuning 仅用 20 篇需要强有力的解释（是 annotation 问题？是计算成本？还是数据 split 问题？）。

---

**[REBUT] R2-W6: Abstract 数字堆砌（~15 个数字）**

部分反驳。Abstract 确实数字密集，但这是一篇以 **量化约束消除** 为核心贡献的方法论文——violation rate 从 X% 降到 Y% 是其 primary claim，不像 conceptual paper 可以用定性语言替代。

我数了 Abstract 中的核心数字：
- Baseline violation: 15.37%, 18.46%, 1-2%（动机）
- LoRA composability: ≤0.20%, ±0.6pp, R-F1 40-46（核心结果）
- Zero-shot: 42-99%, +0.50pp, p<0.001（robustness）
- Beam: R-F1 44.23, 46.11（beam 结果）
- EE: 56 event types, 80 roles, 0%→12-18%（泛化）
- CF: 12%, 21%, ≤1.2%（limitation）

约 20 个数字确实偏多。但我认为问题不在数字数量，而在 **叙事结构**：Abstract 试图同时覆盖 zero-shot、fine-tuned、beam search、EE 四条线，每条都带数字。更好的做法是 Abstract 聚焦 fine-tuning composability（最强结果）+ zero-shot 一句话总结，将 beam search 和 EE 细节留给 Introduction。

所以：数字堆砌的批评方向正确，但建议改的是叙事结构而非简单删数字。

---

**[AGREE] R2-W7: Contribution #3 和 #4 高度重叠**

同意。Contribution #3（§1, line 30）同时包含 "Near-lossless composability with LoRA" 和 "Zero-shot robustness across four models"，已经涵盖了主要实验结果。Contribution #4（line 31）的 CF↔DR trade-off 分析是独立的分析贡献，但 "beam search eliminates all four violation types under fine-tuned decoding" 与 #3 的 composability 叙事重复。合并或重新划分边界是合理的——例如 #3 聚焦系统结果，#4 聚焦分析洞见（tightness pattern + CF mechanism + beam as resolution）。

---

**[REBUT] R2-W8: Table 3 Grammar-only 行 R-F1/E-F1 标粗叙事矛盾**

部分反驳。Table 3（DocRED ablation）的 caption 明确定义了标粗规则："Best per column in **bold** (CF excluded; Tot. included)"。Grammar-only 的 R-F1=8.31 和 E-F1=59.38 确实是该列最高值，标粗遵循了声明的规则。

但 R2 的批评有一定道理：Grammar-only 同时将 DR **增加**到 26.17%（从 18.46%），在 primary metric 上是最差的。读者在扫描表格时，粗体自然吸引注意力，可能误以为 Grammar-only 是"最佳"配置。这是一个 visual communication 问题而非 factual error。

解决方案：可以在 caption 中加一句 clarification，或改为只在 violation rate 列标粗最佳（即 Full S1 行）。这是 minor issue。

---

**[AGREE] R2-W9: DocRED beam search 仅 50 文档子集**

同意，与我自己的 W6 一致。SciERC beam search 用了完整 551 篇但 DocRED 仅 50 篇（Table 10, Appendix D），且 50 篇中 parse failure 升到 6%（3/50）。beam search 在 DocRED 上 DR→0% 的结论基于这 50 篇，统计可靠性不足。论文需说明 50 篇限制的原因（beam search 在长文档上的计算成本？）并要么扩大子集，要么在 claim 中标注 sample size caveat。

---

现在将交叉回应汇报给 Director。
